# Day 38: Production-ready API for your future AI agent
from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException
from pydantic import BaseModel
from typing import List
import asyncio

app = FastAPI(title="AI Agent API", version="1.0")

class AgentRequest(BaseModel):
    prompt: str
    max_tokens: int = 100
    temperature: float = 0.7

class AgentResponse(BaseModel):
    output: str
    tokens_used: int
    agent_name: str

@app.post("/agent/think", response_model=AgentResponse)
async def agent_think(request: AgentRequest):
    """Your AI agent's reasoning endpoint"""
    # Simulate agent processing
    await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
    
    response = f"Agent processed: {request.prompt[:50]}..."
    return AgentResponse(
        output=response,
        tokens_used=len(response.split()),
        agent_name="GPT-4 Agent"
    )

@app.get("/agent/health")
async def health_check():
    return {"status": "ready", "model_loaded": True}

# Run with: uvicorn main:app --reload
Phase 1 Project Deliverable: A production-ready FastAPI server with async endpoints, Pydantic validation, unit tests, and Docker configuration.

# Day 44: Linear Regression from scratch
import numpy as np
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

class LinearRegression:
    def __init__(self, lr=0.01, epochs=1000):
        self.lr = lr
        self.epochs = epochs
        self.weights = None
        self.bias = None
    
    def fit(self, X, y):
        n_samples, n_features = X.shape
        self.weights = np.zeros(n_features)
        self.bias = 0
        
        for _ in range(self.epochs):
            y_pred = X @ self.weights + self.bias
            dw = (1/n_samples) * X.T @ (y_pred - y)
            db = (1/n_samples) * np.sum(y_pred - y)
            
            self.weights -= self.lr * dw
            self.bias -= self.lr * db
    
    def predict(self, X):
        return X @ self.weights + self.bias

# Generate data
X = np.random.randn(100, 1)
y = 3 * X.squeeze() + 2 + np.random.randn(100) * 0.5

model = LinearRegression(lr=0.01, epochs=500)
model.fit(X, y)
print(f"Weight: {model.weights[0]:.2f}, Bias: {model.bias:.2f}")

# Day 54: PyTorch neural network for AI agents
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim

class AgentBrain(nn.Module):
    """Neural network that could power an AI agent's decision making"""
    def __init__(self, input_size=10, hidden_size=64, output_size=4):
        super().__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, hidden_size)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(hidden_size, hidden_size)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(hidden_size, output_size)
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(0.2)
        self.relu = nn.ReLU()
    
    def forward(self, x):
        x = self.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = self.dropout(x)
        x = self.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = self.fc3(x)  # No softmax, CrossEntropyLoss handles it
        return x

# Agent uses this to choose actions
agent = AgentBrain()
dummy_input = torch.randn(1, 10)  # Batch of 1, 10 features
action_logits = agent(dummy_input)
action = torch.argmax(action_logits, dim=1)
print(f"Agent chooses action: {action.item()}")

# Day 64: Self-Attention from scratch (Heart of Transformers)
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

def self_attention(query, key, value, mask=None):
    """
    query, key, value: shape (batch, seq_len, d_model)
    Returns: attention output
    """
    d_k = query.size(-1)
    
    # Step 1: Compute attention scores
    scores = torch.matmul(query, key.transpose(-2, -1)) / torch.sqrt(torch.tensor(d_k))
    
    # Step 2: Apply mask (for padding/future tokens)
    if mask is not None:
        scores = scores.masked_fill(mask == 0, -1e9)
    
    # Step 3: Softmax to get attention weights
    attention_weights = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
    
    # Step 4: Apply to values
    output = torch.matmul(attention_weights, value)
    
    return output, attention_weights

# Example
batch, seq_len, d_model = 2, 5, 512
q = k = v = torch.randn(batch, seq_len, d_model)
output, weights = self_attention(q, k, v)
print(f"Output shape: {output.shape}")  # (2, 5, 512)
print(f"Attention weights shape: {weights.shape}")  # (2, 5, 5)

# Day 75: Complete RAG Pipeline
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
import chromadb
from chromadb.utils import embedding_functions
import numpy as np

class RAGSystem:
    def __init__(self, collection_name="my_docs"):
        self.model = SentenceTransformer('all-MiniLM-L6-v2')
        self.client = chromadb.Client()
        self.collection = self.client.create_collection(
            name=collection_name,
            embedding_function=embedding_functions.SentenceTransformerEmbeddingFunction()
        )
    
    def add_documents(self, texts, metadatas=None):
        ids = [f"doc_{i}" for i in range(len(texts))]
        self.collection.add(
            documents=texts,
            metadatas=metadatas or [{}] * len(texts),
            ids=ids
        )
    
    def retrieve(self, query, k=3):
        results = self.collection.query(query_texts=[query], n_results=k)
        return results['documents'][0]
    
    def generate_answer(self, query, llm_func):
        docs = self.retrieve(query)
        context = "\n".join(docs)
        prompt = f"Context: {context}\n\nQuestion: {query}\nAnswer:"
        return llm_func(prompt)  # Your LLM call here

# Usage
rag = RAGSystem()
rag.add_documents([
    "Python is a programming language created by Guido van Rossum in 1991.",
    "AsyncIO enables concurrent programming in Python.",
    "AI agents use language models to make decisions."
])

results = rag.retrieve("What is Python?")
print(results)  # Returns relevant document

# Day 88: ReAct Pattern from scratch
import re

class ReActAgent:
    def __init__(self, llm, tools):
        self.llm = llm
        self.tools = {tool.name: tool for tool in tools}
    
    def run(self, question, max_steps=5):
        thought_history = []
        current_question = question
        
        for step in range(max_steps):
            prompt = self._build_prompt(current_question, thought_history)
            response = self.llm.generate(prompt)
            
            # Parse Thought/Action/Observation
            thought = self._extract(response, "Thought")
            action = self._extract(response, "Action")
            action_input = self._extract(response, "Action Input")
            
            if action == "Finish":
                return self._extract(response, "Final Answer")
            
            # Execute tool
            if action in self.tools:
                observation = self.tools[action].run(action_input)
            else:
                observation = f"Error: Tool {action} not found"
            
            thought_history.append({
                "thought": thought,
                "action": action,
                "input": action_input,
                "observation": observation
            })
            
            current_question = f"Observation: {observation}"
        
        return "Max steps reached"
    
    def _build_prompt(self, question, history):
        # ReAct prompt template
        prompt = f"Question: {question}\n"
        for step in history:
            prompt += f"Thought: {step['thought']}\n"
            prompt += f"Action: {step['action']}\n"
            prompt += f"Action Input: {step['input']}\n"
            prompt += f"Observation: {step['observation']}\n"
        prompt += "Thought:"
        return prompt

python
# Day 115: LangGraph conditional agent (Modern agent framework)
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, END
from typing import TypedDict, Literal

class AgentState(TypedDict):
    messages: list
    next_step: str
    context: dict

def router_node(state: AgentState) -> Literal["search", "calculate", "respond"]:
    """Decide what to do next"""
    last_message = state["messages"][-1].lower()
    
    if "search" in last_message or "find" in last_message:
        return "search"
    elif "calculate" in last_message or "math" in last_message:
        return "calculate"
    else:
        return "respond"

def search_node(state: AgentState):
    # Implement web search
    state["context"]["search_results"] = "Results found"
    state["messages"].append("Search executed")
    return state

def calculate_node(state: AgentState):
    # Implement calculator
    state["context"]["calculation"] = 42
    state["messages"].append("Calculation done")
    return state

def respond_node(state: AgentState):
    state["messages"].append("Final response generated")
    return state

# Build the graph
builder = StateGraph(AgentState)
builder.add_node("search", search_node)
builder.add_node("calculate", calculate_node)
builder.add_node("respond", respond_node)
builder.add_conditional_edges("router", router_node)
builder.set_entry_point("router")

graph = builder.compile()

python
# Day 120: CrewAI Multi-Agent System
from crewai import Agent, Task, Crew, Process
from langchain.tools import DuckDuckGoSearchRun

# Researcher Agent
researcher = Agent(
    role='Senior Research Analyst',
    goal='Find comprehensive information on any topic',
    backstory='Expert researcher with 20 years experience',
    tools=[DuckDuckGoSearchRun()],
    verbose=True
)

# Writer Agent
writer = Agent(
    role='Technical Writer',
    goal='Create clear, engaging content from research',
    backstory='Award-winning technical writer',
    verbose=True
)

# Critic Agent
critic = Agent(
    role='Quality Assurance Editor',
    goal='Ensure accuracy and quality of content',
    backstory='Detail-oriented editor',
    verbose=True
)

# Tasks
research_task = Task(
    description="Research quantum computing breakthroughs in 2024",
    agent=researcher,
    expected_output="Detailed research findings"
)

writing_task = Task(
    description="Write an article based on research",
    agent=writer,
    expected_output="Well-structured article"
)

review_task = Task(
    description="Review and improve the article",
    agent=critic,
    expected_output="Final polished article"
)

# Crew
my_crew = Crew(
    agents=[researcher, writer, critic],
    tasks=[research_task, writing_task, review_task],
    process=Process.sequential
)

# Run
result = my_crew.kickoff()
print(result)

python
# Day 145: Training a robot with PPO
import gymnasium as gym
from stable_baselines3 import PPO
from stable_baselines3.common.env_util import make_vec_env

# Create robotic environment
env = gym.make("LunarLander-v2", render_mode="rgb_array")

# Train the robot's brain
model = PPO(
    "MlpPolicy", 
    env, 
    verbose=1,
    learning_rate=0.0003,
    n_steps=2048,
    batch_size=64,
    gamma=0.99
)

print("Training robot...")
model.learn(total_timesteps=100000)
model.save("robot_lander")

# Test the trained robot
env = gym.make("LunarLander-v2", render_mode="human")
obs, _ = env.reset()
for _ in range(1000):
    action, _ = model.predict(obs, deterministic=True)
    obs, reward, terminated, truncated, _ = env.step(action)
    if terminated or truncated:
        obs, _ = env.reset()
env.close()

python
# Day 156: Creating entanglement - Quantum's "secret sauce"
from qiskit import QuantumCircuit, execute, Aer
from qiskit.visualization import plot_histogram

# Create Bell state (|00⟩ + |11⟩)/√2
qc = QuantumCircuit(2, 2)
qc.h(0)      # Hadamard on qubit 0
qc.cx(0, 1)  # CNOT with control=0, target=1
qc.measure([0, 1], [0, 1])

# Simulate
backend = Aer.get_backend('qasm_simulator')
job = execute(qc, backend, shots=1024)
result = job.result()
counts = result.get_counts(qc)

print("Measurement results:", counts)
# Output should show only '00' and '11' (entangled states)
# plot_histogram(counts)

Quick Decision Framework

1. WHAT IS AN AI AGENT?
Concept Definition
An AI Agent is an autonomous system that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to perceive its environment, reason about goals, take actions using tools, and learn from memory - all with minimal human intervention.

Where It's Used
Industry	Use Case	Example
Customer Support	24/7 ticket resolution	Airline baggage claim agent
Software Development	Code generation & debugging	GitHub Copilot agent
Research	Literature review & synthesis	Perplexity AI research agent
Finance	Market analysis & trading	Bloomberg GPT agent
Healthcare	Symptom checking & scheduling	Mayo Clinic chatbot agent
Sales	Lead qualification & outreach	Salesforce Einstein agent
Agent vs Agentic AI - The Debate (From Your Image)
text
November 2024: "Your n8n workflows are not autonomous enough. Stop calling them AI agents 1.0"
June 2025: "Those are AI agents, not agentic AI. Only agentic AI can be fully agentic."

MY TAKE: Don't waste time on academic debates. What matters to users is actual behavior.
Example Scenario: Customer Support Agent
python
# Traditional Chatbot (NOT an agent)
def traditional_chatbot(user_query):
    responses = {
        "refund": "Please contact refunds@company.com",
        "shipping": "Orders ship in 3-5 days"
    }
    return responses.get(user_query, "I don't understand")

# True AI Agent (WITH autonomy, tools, memory)
class CustomerSupportAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.tools = {
            "check_order_status": self.check_order_api,
            "process_refund": self.refund_api,
            "escalate_to_human": self.helpdesk_api
        }
        self.memory = ConversationMemory(window=10)
    
    def handle_query(self, user_message, user_id):
        # 1. Retrieve past interactions
        history = self.memory.get_user_history(user_id)
        
        # 2. Reason about intent
        intent = self.llm.classify(user_message, history)
        
        # 3. Take autonomous action
        if intent == "refund_request":
            order_id = self.extract_order_id(user_message)
            refund_status = self.tools["process_refund"](order_id)
            return f"Refund of ${refund_status.amount} processed"
        
        # 4. Learn from outcome
        self.memory.store_outcome(user_id, intent, "success")
        
        return self.llm.generate_response(user_message, history)
2. HOW AI AGENTS WORK - COMPLETE ARCHITECTURE
The 7-Step Process (From Your Image)
text
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ STEP 1: SYSTEM PROMPT                                       │
│ ├── Goals: What the agent must achieve                      │
│ ├── Role: Persona/identity (e.g., "Expert researcher")      │
│ └── Instructions: Rules and constraints                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ STEP 2: LLM (Base Model)                                    │
│ ├── GPT-4o, Claude 3.5, Gemini 1.5 Pro, Llama 3             │
│ ├── Parameters: Temperature, Top-P, Max Tokens              │
│ └── Fine-tuning options                                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ STEP 3: TOOLS                                               │
│ ├── Simple: Calculator, File I/O, Code execution            │
│ ├── API: Web search, Database, Slack, Email                 │
│ ├── MCP Server: Model Context Protocol                      │
│ └── AI Agent as Tool: Nested agents                         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ STEP 4: MEMORY                                              │
│ ├── Episodic: Conversation history                          │
│ ├── Working: Current task context                           │
│ ├── Vector DB: Long-term knowledge                          │
│ ├── SQL DB: Structured data                                 │
│ └── File store: Documents, images                           │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ STEP 5: ORCHESTRATION                                       │
│ ├── Routes/Workflows                                        │
│ ├── Triggers (schedule, webhook, queue)                     │
│ ├── Message queues (RabbitMQ, Redis)                        │
│ └── Agent-to-agent communication                            │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ STEP 6: UI INTERFACE                                        │
│ ├── Chat UI, Dashboard, API endpoint                        │
│ ├── Annotation Mode (visual editing)                        │
│ └── Live preview & iteration                                │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ STEP 7: AI EVALS                                            │
│ ├── Analyze: Success/failure patterns                       │
│ ├── Measure: Accuracy, latency, cost                        │
│ └── Improve: Prompt optimization, fine-tuning               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Complete Agent Code Example
python
# Complete production-ready agent with all 7 steps

from typing import Dict, List, Any
import json
from datetime import datetime

class CompleteAIAgent:
    def __init__(self, config: Dict):
        # STEP 1: System Prompt
        self.system_prompt = {
            "role": "Senior Financial Analyst",
            "goals": [
                "Analyze market trends",
                "Identify investment opportunities",
                "Generate risk-adjusted recommendations"
            ],
            "instructions": """
            - Always verify data from at least 2 sources
            - Flag any recommendation with >15% risk
            - Provide confidence scores for all predictions
            - Use technical analysis tools when available
            """
        }
        
        # STEP 2: LLM Configuration
        self.llm_config = {
            "model": config.get("model", "gpt-4o"),
            "temperature": 0.3,  # Low for consistency
            "max_tokens": 4096,
            "top_p": 0.95
        }
        
        # STEP 3: Tools
        self.tools = {
            "market_data": self.fetch_market_data,
            "financial_news": self.scrape_news,
            "risk_calculator": self.calculate_var,
            "portfolio_api": self.get_portfolio_holdings
        }
        
        # STEP 4: Memory (Tiered)
        self.memory = {
            "working": [],  # Current analysis context
            "episodic": self.load_conversation_history(),
            "long_term": VectorStore("financial_knowledge"),
            "cache": RedisClient(ttl=3600)
        }
        
        # STEP 5: Orchestration
        self.orchestration = {
            "workflow": "analysis_pipeline",
            "max_steps": 5,
            "fallback": "escalate_to_human"
        }
        
        # STEP 6: UI (API endpoint)
        self.api_endpoint = "/api/v1/analyze"
        
        # STEP 7: Evals
        self.metrics = {
            "accuracy_history": [],
            "latency_tracking": [],
            "cost_per_query": 0.0
        }
    
    async def analyze(self, query: str, user_id: str) -> Dict:
        """Main execution loop"""
        start_time = datetime.now()
        
        # Check cache
        cache_key = f"{user_id}:{hash(query)}"
        cached = self.memory["cache"].get(cache_key)
        if cached:
            return cached
        
        # Build context from memory
        history = self.memory["episodic"].get_last_n(user_id, n=5)
        knowledge = self.memory["long_term"].search(query, top_k=3)
        
        # LLM reasoning
        response = await self.llm_call(query, history, knowledge)
        
        # Tool execution if needed
        if response.get("needs_tool"):
            tool_result = await self.execute_tool(response["tool_name"])
            response = await self.llm_call(query, history, knowledge, tool_result)
        
        # Update memory
        self.memory["episodic"].add(user_id, {"query": query, "response": response})
        self.memory["cache"].set(cache_key, response, ttl=300)
        
        # Track metrics
        elapsed = (datetime.now() - start_time).total_seconds()
        self.metrics["latency_tracking"].append(elapsed)
        
        return response

Scenario: Investment firm needs daily market briefing

Without Agent:
  - Analyst manually checks 5 sources (2 hours)
  - Writes summary (1 hour)
  - Emails to team
  - Total: 3 hours/day

With Agent (Complete 7-step):
  - Agent fetches data via APIs (2 minutes)
  - Analyzes trends using LLM (30 seconds)
  - Generates report with citations (1 minute)
  - Emails to team automatically
  - Total: 3.5 minutes
  
ROI: 50x productivity gain
3. AI MEMORY SYSTEMS - 8 TYPES EXPLAINED
From Your Image: "AI Agent Memory Is a Stack — Not a Vector DB"
The Complete Memory Stack
text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     MEMORY LAYER 1                              │
│  CONTEXT WINDOW MEMORY (Inside LLM)                            │
│  ├── What: Token budget for immediate reasoning                │
│  ├── Where: LLM internal                                       │
│  ├── Use Case: Current conversation turn                       │
│  └── Limit: 80% capped to prevent overflow                     │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 2                              │
│  CONVERSATION BUFFER (Agent Runtime)                           │
│  ├── What: Dialogue history across turns                       │
│  ├── Where: Agent state                                        │
│  ├── Use Case: Multi-turn continuity                           │
│  └── Tech: Redis, In-memory store                              │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 3                              │
│  SEMANTIC MEMORY (Memory Service)                              │
│  ├── What: User knowledge, preferences, facts                  │
│  ├── Where: MemO, Vector DB                                    │
│  ├── Use Case: Long-term recall, personalization               │
│  └── Example: "User prefers short answers"                     │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 4                              │
│  EPISODIC MEMORY (Memory Traces)                               │
│  ├── What: Past successes and failures                         │
│  ├── Where: Interaction logs                                   │
│  ├── Use Case: Learning from outcomes                          │
│  └── Example: "Last refund request failed - try different approach"│
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 5                              │
│  TOOL RESPONSE CACHE (Redis)                                   │
│  ├── What: API results deduplication                           │
│  ├── Where: In-memory cache                                    │
│  ├── Use Case: Cost + latency control                          │
│  └── Benefit: <1ms retrieval, 40% lower API costs             │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 6                              │
│  RAG CACHE (Vector Store)                                      │
│  ├── What: Embedding reuse                                     │
│  ├── Where: Pinecone, Weaviate, Chroma                         │
│  ├── Use Case: Retrieval efficiency                            │
│  └── Benefit: Query deduplication                              │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 7                              │
│  AGENT STATE STORE (Workflow Engine)                           │
│  ├── What: State snapshots for debugging                       │
│  ├── Where: LangGraph + Redis                                  │
│  ├── Use Case: Time-travel debugging, replay                   │
│  └── Benefit: 70% faster debugging                             │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     MEMORY LAYER 8                              │
│  PROCEDURAL MEMORY (Agent Architecture)                        │
│  ├── What: Guardrails, node logic, execution rules             │
│  ├── Where: Baked into LangGraph nodes                         │
│  ├── Use Case: Consistent behavior                             │
│  └── Example: "Always verify before acting"                    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Complete Memory Implementation
python
# Production-ready memory stack (8 layers)

from typing import Dict, List, Any, Optional
import redis
import chromadb
from datetime import datetime, timedelta

class AgentMemoryStack:
    def __init__(self):
        # Layer 1: Context Window (managed by LLM)
        self.context_limit = 8000  # tokens
        self.context_reserve = 0.2  # 20% for tool responses
        
        # Layer 2: Conversation Buffer
        self.conversation_buffers: Dict[str, List] = {}
        
        # Layer 3: Semantic Memory (User knowledge)
        self.semantic_store = ChromaClient(collection="user_preferences")
        
        # Layer 4: Episodic Memory (Past interactions)
        self.episodic_store: Dict[str, List[Dict]] = {}
        
        # Layer 5: Tool Response Cache
        self.tool_cache = redis.Redis(host='localhost', decode_responses=True)
        self.cache_ttl = 3600  # 1 hour
        
        # Layer 6: RAG Cache
        self.rag_cache = {}
        self.embedding_cache = {}
        
        # Layer 7: Agent State Store
        self.state_store = redis.Redis(host='localhost', db=1)
        
        # Layer 8: Procedural Memory (Guardrails)
        self.procedural_rules = self.load_guardrails()
    
    # Layer 1 & 2: Context Management
    def prepare_context(self, user_id: str, current_input: str) -> str:
        """Prepare context window with conversation history"""
        history = self.conversation_buffers.get(user_id, [])
        
        # Keep last N turns within token limit
        context_tokens = self.count_tokens(str(history))
        while context_tokens > self.context_limit * (1 - self.context_reserve):
            history.pop(0)
            context_tokens = self.count_tokens(str(history))
        
        return "\n".join(history) + f"\nUser: {current_input}"
    
    # Layer 3: Semantic Memory (Personalization)
    def remember_user_preference(self, user_id: str, preference: Dict):
        """Store user preferences for long-term recall"""
        self.semantic_store.upsert(
            documents=[preference["value"]],
            metadatas=[{"user_id": user_id, "type": preference["type"]}],
            ids=[f"{user_id}_{preference['type']}"]
        )
    
    def get_user_context(self, user_id: str) -> Dict:
        """Retrieve user knowledge across sessions"""
        results = self.semantic_store.query(
            query_texts=["user context"],
            filter={"user_id": user_id}
        )
        return self.aggregate_preferences(results)
    
    # Layer 4: Episodic Memory (Learning)
    def record_outcome(self, user_id: str, action: str, success: bool, details: Dict):
        """Store interaction outcomes for learning"""
        episode = {
            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "action": action,
            "success": success,
            "details": details,
            "user_id": user_id
        }
        
        if user_id not in self.episodic_store:
            self.episodic_store[user_id] = []
        
        self.episodic_store[user_id].append(episode)
        
        # Keep last 100 episodes per user
        if len(self.episodic_store[user_id]) > 100:
            self.episodic_store[user_id] = self.episodic_store[user_id][-100:]
    
    def learn_from_history(self, user_id: str) -> Dict:
        """Extract patterns from past interactions"""
        episodes = self.episodic_store.get(user_id, [])
        if not episodes:
            return {}
        
        # Calculate success rates by action type
        action_stats = {}
        for ep in episodes:
            action = ep["action"]
            if action not in action_stats:
                action_stats[action] = {"success": 0, "total": 0}
            action_stats[action]["total"] += 1
            if ep["success"]:
                action_stats[action]["success"] += 1
        
        # Identify best strategies
        return {
            action: stats["success"] / stats["total"]
            for action, stats in action_stats.items()
        }
    
    # Layer 5: Tool Response Cache
    def cache_tool_response(self, tool_name: str, params: Dict, response: Any):
        """Cache API responses to reduce costs"""
        cache_key = f"{tool_name}:{hash(str(params))}"
        self.tool_cache.setex(cache_key, self.cache_ttl, json.dumps(response))
    
    def get_cached_tool_response(self, tool_name: str, params: Dict) -> Optional[Any]:
        """Retrieve cached API response"""
        cache_key = f"{tool_name}:{hash(str(params))}"
        cached = self.tool_cache.get(cache_key)
        return json.loads(cached) if cached else None
    
    # Layer 6: RAG Cache
    def cache_embedding(self, text: str, embedding: List[float]):
        """Cache embeddings for reuse"""
        text_hash = hash(text)
        self.embedding_cache[text_hash] = embedding
    
    def get_embedding(self, text: str) -> Optional[List[float]]:
        """Retrieve cached embedding"""
        text_hash = hash(text)
        return self.embedding_cache.get(text_hash)
    
    # Layer 7: Agent State Store (Debugging)
    def save_state(self, execution_id: str, step: int, state: Dict):
        """Save agent state for time-travel debugging"""
        key = f"agent_state:{execution_id}:step_{step}"
        self.state_store.setex(key, timedelta(days=7), json.dumps(state))
    
    def replay_state(self, execution_id: str, step: int) -> Optional[Dict]:
        """Replay agent state for debugging"""
        key = f"agent_state:{execution_id}:step_{step}"
        state = self.state_store.get(key)
        return json.loads(state) if state else None
    
    # Layer 8: Procedural Memory (Guardrails)
    def load_guardrails(self) -> List[Dict]:
        """Load procedural rules for consistent behavior"""
        return [
            {
                "name": "no_personal_data",
                "rule": "Never store credit card numbers",
                "action": "redact"
            },
            {
                "name": "max_refund",
                "rule": "Refunds over $100 require approval",
                "action": "escalate"
            },
            {
                "name": "rate_limit",
                "rule": "Max 10 tool calls per minute",
                "action": "throttle"
            }
        ]
    
    def enforce_guardrails(self, action: Dict) -> Dict:
        """Apply procedural memory before execution"""
        for guardrail in self.procedural_rules:
            if self.violates_rule(action, guardrail):
                return {
                    "allowed": False,
                    "reason": guardrail["name"],
                    "suggested_action": guardrail["action"]
                }
        return {"allowed": True}
Scenario: E-commerce Support Agent Using All 8 Memory Layers
python
# Real-world example: Customer returns to chat after 2 weeks

class EcommerceMemoryAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.memory = AgentMemoryStack()
    
    async def handle_returning_customer(self, user_id: str, query: str):
        # Layer 1-2: Context Window - Loads recent chat
        context = self.memory.prepare_context(user_id, query)
        
        # Layer 3: Semantic Memory - Recalls user preferences
        preferences = self.memory.get_user_context(user_id)
        # Output: {"style": "concise", "product_category": "electronics"}
        
        # Layer 4: Episodic Memory - Remembers last issue
        past_episodes = self.memory.learn_from_history(user_id)
        # Output: {"refund_requests": 0.8 success rate, "returns": 0.95 success rate}
        
        # Layer 5: Tool Cache - Reuses previous API calls
        cached_order = self.memory.get_cached_tool_response("get_order", {"user_id": user_id})
        
        # Layer 6: RAG Cache - Reuses embeddings
        cached_knowledge = self.memory.get_embedding(query)
        
        # Layer 7: State Store - Can debug previous session if needed
        previous_state = self.memory.replay_state(f"session_{user_id}", step=3)
        
        # Layer 8: Procedural Memory - Apply rules
        guardrail_check = self.memory.enforce_guardrails({"action": "process_refund"})
        
        return {
            "context_aware": True,
            "personalized": True,
            "cost_saved": "60%",
            "debuggable": True,
            "safe": guardrail_check["allowed"]
        }

Deep Dive: Each Framework with Examples
1. LangGraph - Production Stateful Agents
python
# When to use: Complex workflows with human-in-the-loop, multi-step reasoning
# Where used: Customer support escalation, legal document review, medical diagnosis

from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, END
from langgraph.checkpoint import MemorySaver
from typing import TypedDict, Literal

class SupportState(TypedDict):
    messages: list
    current_tier: Literal["tier1", "tier2", "tier3"]
    resolution: str
    human_needed: bool

class LangGraphSupportAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = StateGraph(SupportState)
        self._build_support_workflow()
        self.checkpointer = MemorySaver()
        self.app = self.graph.compile(checkpointer=self.checkpointer)
    
    def _build_support_workflow(self):
        # Nodes
        self.graph.add_node("triage", self.triage_issue)
        self.graph.add_node("tier1_solve", self.tier1_automated)
        self.graph.add_node("tier2_review", self.tier2_human_like)
        self.graph.add_node("tier3_escalate", self.escalate_to_human)
        self.graph.add_node("synthesize", self.synthesize_response)
        
        # Edges with routing
        self.graph.set_entry_point("triage")
        self.graph.add_conditional_edges(
            "triage",
            self.route_complexity,
            {
                "simple": "tier1_solve",
                "medium": "tier2_review", 
                "complex": "tier3_escalate"
            }
        )
        self.graph.add_edge("tier1_solve", "synthesize")
        self.graph.add_edge("tier2_review", "synthesize")
        self.graph.add_edge("tier3_escalate", "synthesize")
        self.graph.add_edge("synthesize", END)
    
    async def triage_issue(self, state: SupportState):
        """Analyze issue complexity"""
        last_message = state["messages"][-1]
        
        # Use LLM to classify
        complexity = await self.classify_complexity(last_message)
        
        return {
            "current_tier": complexity,
            "messages": state["messages"] + [{"role": "system", "content": f"Complexity: {complexity}"}]
        }
    
    async def tier1_solve(self, state: SupportState):
        """Fully automated resolution"""
        # Use RAG from knowledge base
        solution = await self.rag_search(state["messages"][-1])
        return {"resolution": solution}
    
    async def escalate_to_human(self, state: SupportState):
        """Human-in-the-loop for complex issues"""
        # Create ticket and notify human agent
        ticket_id = await self.create_support_ticket(state)
        return {"human_needed": True, "resolution": f"Ticket #{ticket_id} created"}
    
    async def process(self, user_message: str, thread_id: str):
        config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
        
        result = await self.app.ainvoke(
            {"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": user_message}]},
            config=config
        )
        
        return result["resolution"]
2. CrewAI - Role-Based Team Collaboration
python
# When to use: Multi-expert collaboration, content creation, research teams
# Where used: Marketing campaign creation, software development teams, research papers

from crewai import Agent, Task, Crew, Process
from crewai.tools import tool

class ContentCreationCrew:
    """Marketing content team with specialized roles"""
    
    @tool("search_trends")
    def search_marketing_trends(topic: str) -> str:
        """Search current marketing trends"""
        # API call to trend database
        return f"Top trends in {topic}: AI personalization, short-form video, UGC"
    
    def __init__(self):
        # Role 1: Strategist
        self.strategist = Agent(
            role="Content Strategist",
            goal="Develop winning content strategy based on trends",
            backstory="10 years in digital marketing with focus on viral content",
            tools=[self.search_marketing_trends],
            verbose=True,
            allow_delegation=True
        )
        
        # Role 2: Writer
        self.writer = Agent(
            role="Senior Copywriter",
            goal="Create engaging, on-brand content",
            backstory="Award-winning copywriter with expertise in storytelling",
            verbose=True,
            allow_delegation=False
        )
        
        # Role 3: Editor
        self.editor = Agent(
            role="Content Editor",
            goal="Ensure quality, accuracy, and brand voice consistency",
            backstory="Former NY Times editor with eagle eye for detail",
            verbose=True,
            allow_delegation=True
        )
        
        # Role 4: SEO Specialist
        self.seo = Agent(
            role="SEO Specialist",
            goal="Optimize content for search engines",
            backstory="SEO expert who has ranked 100+ pages on Google page 1",
            verbose=True,
            allow_delegation=False
        )
    
    def create_campaign(self, topic: str, brand_voice: str):
        # Task 1: Research
        research_task = Task(
            description=f"Research {topic} and identify key angles, audience pain points",
            agent=self.strategist,
            expected_output="List of 5 content angles with supporting data"
        )
        
        # Task 2: Write
        writing_task = Task(
            description=f"Write blog post about {topic} in {brand_voice} voice. Include hook, body, CTA",
            agent=self.writer,
            expected_output="1500-word blog post ready for editing"
        )
        
        # Task 3: SEO
        seo_task = Task(
            description=f"Add SEO metadata: title tag, meta description, keywords, internal links",
            agent=self.seo,
            expected_output="SEO-optimized version of the blog post"
        )
        
        # Task 4: Edit
        edit_task = Task(
            description="Review and polish content for grammar, flow, and brand consistency",
            agent=self.editor,
            expected_output="Final published-ready blog post",
            context=[research_task, writing_task, seo_task]
        )
        
        # Assemble crew
        content_crew = Crew(
            agents=[self.strategist, self.writer, self.seo, self.editor],
            tasks=[research_task, writing_task, seo_task, edit_task],
            process=Process.sequential,  # Tasks executed in order
            verbose=2
        )
        
        result = content_crew.kickoff()
        return result

# Usage
crew = ContentCreationCrew()
blog_post = crew.create_campaign(
    topic="AI in Customer Service 2025",
    brand_voice="Friendly, expert, slightly humorous"
)
3. AutoGen - Conversational Multi-Agent
python
# When to use: Complex problem-solving, code generation, research collaboration
# Where used: Software development teams, scientific research, data analysis

import autogen
from autogen import AssistantAgent, UserProxyAgent, GroupChat, GroupChatManager

class AutoGenResearchTeam:
    """Multi-agent research system with specialized roles"""
    
    def __init__(self, config_list):
        # Agent 1: Planner
        self.planner = AssistantAgent(
            name="Planner",
            system_message="""You are a research planner. Break down complex questions into 
            subtasks. Assign tasks to appropriate specialists. Coordinate the team.""",
            llm_config={"config_list": config_list}
        )
        
        # Agent 2: Researcher
        self.researcher = AssistantAgent(
            name="Researcher",
            system_message="""You are a researcher expert in finding and synthesizing information.
            Use web search, academic databases, and news sources. Always cite sources.""",
            llm_config={"config_list": config_list},
            function_map={"web_search": self.web_search}
        )
        
        # Agent 3: Analyst
        self.analyst = AssistantAgent(
            name="DataAnalyst",
            system_message="""You analyze data, create visualizations, and find patterns.
            Use Python, pandas, matplotlib for analysis. Provide statistical insights.""",
            llm_config={"config_list": config_list}
        )
        
        # Agent 4: Writer
        self.writer = AssistantAgent(
            name="Writer",
            system_message="""You synthesize findings into clear, compelling reports.
            Structure with executive summary, methodology, findings, conclusions.""",
            llm_config={"config_list": config_list}
        )
        
        # Human proxy for approval
        self.user_proxy = UserProxyAgent(
            name="Human_Reviewer",
            human_input_mode="TERMINATE",  # Human reviews final output
            code_execution_config={"work_dir": "research_output"}
        )
    
    async def research_topic(self, query: str):
        """Orchestrate multi-agent research"""
        
        # Create group chat
        group_chat = GroupChat(
            agents=[self.user_proxy, self.planner, self.researcher, self.analyst, self.writer],
            messages=[],
            max_round=20  # Prevent infinite loops
        )
        
        manager = GroupChatManager(
            groupchat=group_chat,
            llm_config={"config_list": config_list}
        )
        
        # Initiate research
        result = await self.user_proxy.initiate_chat(
            manager,
            message=f"""
            Research topic: {query}
            
            Process:
            1. Planner: Break down into 3 sub-questions
            2. Researcher: Find information for each sub-question
            3. Analyst: Analyze findings and create 2 visualizations
            4. Writer: Synthesize into final report
            5. Human_Reviewer: Approve or request changes
            """
        )
        
        return result
4. n8n - Workflow Automation
python
# When to use: Business process automation, integrations, scheduled tasks
# Where used: Lead nurturing, data pipelines, alerting systems

# n8n workflow in Python (equivalent using Prefect/Temporal)

from prefect import flow, task
from prefect.tasks import task_input_hash
from datetime import timedelta

class N8NStyleWorkflow:
    """Visual workflow automation equivalent"""
    
    @task(cache_key_fn=task_input_hash, cache_expiration=timedelta(hours=1))
    def fetch_leads(self, source: str):
        """Trigger: New lead from CRM"""
        # API call to Salesforce/HubSpot
        return {"leads": [{"id": 1, "email": "john@example.com", "score": 85}]}
    
    @task
    def enrich_lead(self, lead: dict):
        """Action: Enrich with LinkedIn data"""
        # Call LinkedIn API
        lead["company"] = "Tech Corp"
        lead["title"] = "CTO"
        return lead
    
    @task
    def score_lead(self, lead: dict):
        """Logic: Score based on fit"""
        lead["final_score"] = lead["score"] * 1.2 if lead["title"] == "CTO" else lead["score"]
        return lead
    
    @task
    def route_lead(self, lead: dict):
        """Router: Send to appropriate sales rep"""
        if lead["final_score"] > 90:
            channel = "enterprise_slack"
        elif lead["final_score"] > 70:
            channel = "midmarket_slack"
        else:
            channel = "nurture_email"
        return {"lead": lead, "channel": channel}
    
    @task
    def notify_sales(self, lead: dict, channel: str):
        """Action: Send notification"""
        # Slack webhook
        print(f"Lead {lead['id']} routed to {channel}")
    
    @flow(name="lead_management_pipeline")
    def lead_pipeline(self, source: str = "salesforce"):
        """Complete workflow"""
        leads = self.fetch_leads(source)
        
        for lead in leads["leads"]:
            enriched = self.enrich_lead(lead)
            scored = self.score_lead(enriched)
            routed = self.route_lead(scored)
            self.notify_sales(r

	🏛️ Sovereign AI - Core Definition
Sovereign AI refers to a nation's or organization's capacity to independently develop, deploy, and govern artificial intelligence using its own infrastructure, data, models, and talent . It represents the ability to retain full control over the entire AI lifecycle—from physical compute to algorithmic logic .

Key Dimensions of Sovereign AI :
Dimension	Description
Territorial	Where data and compute physically reside
Operational	Who manages and secures data and compute
Technological	Who owns the underlying stack and IP
Legal	Which jurisdiction governs access and compliance
